### 6.16 Registry-v1 schema-owner review pack (2026-10-27, appended; DDL NOT applied)
Draft: `sveltekit-frontend/drizzle/manual/20261020_atlas_packet_identity_registry_v1_DRAFT.sql` (no seed rows). Rehearsal today on a throwaway `postgres:18` container (removed afterwards; live DB untouched): applies cleanly inside a transaction; a `repo:root` row inserts; a `repo:turbovec` row is rejected by the root CHECK; a second row for the same (scope, source_ref, kind) is rejected by the unique key; after `ROLLBACK` zero registry tables remain. Fixture rows only.
Decisions the schema owner must make before any apply (each with the consequence):
1. **Root-only CHECK** (`membership_repository_scope = 'repo:root'`): keeps the 6 unresolved nested scopes unregistrable until each has a persisted `atlas_repository_identity` row. Consequence: 1,079 files stay without registry identity. Alternative (drop the CHECK, rely on the FK plus the (repository_id, source_ref, kind) key) is weaker because scope labels are strings.
2. **No UUID in SQL** (agreed): `repository_id` is a FK to `atlas_repository_identity`; the scope-to-UUID mapping is resolved from persisted rows by `repository-scope-resolution-core.mjs`, so the DB cannot yet check that scope and repository_id agree. Option: add a persisted scope column on `atlas_repository_identity` (a separate DDL decision).
3. **Alias cardinality**: `UNIQUE (canonical_packet_key)` makes each canonical key own one storage locator. Allowing historical extras needs an evidence-reviewed second alias kind, not a relaxed constraint.
4. **Legacy table** `atlas_packet_identity_aliases` (3,294 rows, 7 readers, see 6.10) is untouched and stays authoritative for those readers until parity (`alias-parity-core.mjs`) is run live and clean.
5. **Authority prerequisite**: even with the DDL applied, the admission builder rejects every entry while execution `01a1763a…` is `COMPLETED/false`; the DDL does not unblock admission by itself.
Apply procedure once approved (not run): manual `psql` of the file in one transaction, readback of both tables' constraints, record in this file; rollback is `DROP TABLE` of the two empty tables only while they hold zero rows.

### 6.M4 Canonical relation storage and binary graph design (2026-10-27, appended)
Target `CANONICAL_RELATION_STORAGE_DESIGN_VERIFIED` for P0, P1, P2, P4, P5 only. NOT reached: P3 (exporter owned by another session), P6 beyond a design note. Not claimed: `RELATIONS_PERSISTED`, `NEO4J_PROJECTED`, `SAFE_TO_PROJECT`. First failing canonical admission predicate: `REGISTRY_DDL_NOT_APPLIED`, then `GRAPHIFY_EXECUTION_NOT_CANONICAL`. No DDL, writes or index creation on the live DB.
- **P0 existing-table reuse (live PG18, read-only):** `atlas_relationships` (603 rows, all `USES_CONCEPT`, all with `source_revision`, all with evidence) is the canonical fact table; `atlas_relationship_evidence` (603; columns only relationship_id, evidence_id, confidence) holds observations; `atlas_graph_edges_v2` (224,764 rows over 2 snapshots: CONTAINS 112,382, MATERIALIZES 58,304, DERIVED_FROM 54,078) is a derived snapshot edge store; `atlas_projection_outbox` and `atlas_projection_state` (both 0 rows) are the outbox owners to reuse, so no new outbox; `atlas_graph_relation_events_v2`, `atlas_graph_relation_participants_v2`, `atlas_typed_edges` are empty; `atlas_symbol_registry` (42,922) and `atlas_repository_identity` (1) own symbol and repository identity. Gaps: no snapshot/scope column on `atlas_relationships`; evidence rows lack evidence revision, extraction recipe and span; zero projections have ever run.
- **P1 contract:** `scripts/atlas/lib/canonical-relation-contract-v1.mjs` (+ test, 8 tests). Endpoint-class allow-list with direction, UUID repository/snapshot scope or declared cross-repository scope, mandatory recipe + sha256 relation revision, at least one evidence item of a kind independent of the extractor, logical identity that excludes evidence and confidence, insert-or-verify returning INSERT / VERIFIED_EXISTING / CONFLICT / REJECT. LLM, embedding-similarity, k-means, SOM-neighbor and heuristic origins return PROPOSAL_ONLY and are never admissible.
- **P2 index analysis (read-only):** pattern "outgoing typed neighbors" uses `atlas_graph_edges_v2_forward_typed_idx` (index-only, ~2 ms, shared hit=3 read=3); "incoming typed" uses `…_reverse_typed_idx` (index-only; 35.8 ms was dominated by cold reads and my own subquery); JSONB containment on `atlas_relationships.metadata` uses the GIN index (2.3 ms, table is tiny). `pg_stat_user_indexes` shows idx_scan 0 for forward/reverse typed, source_idx (27 MB) and pkey, 2 for target_idx (18 MB): counters may have been reset recently, so no index is proposed for removal; `source_idx` and `target_idx` look redundant with the typed pair and are a review candidate only. Column correlation on the edge table is low (|r| <= 0.50), so BRIN is not justified there. No draft SQL written because no measured gap exists.
- **P4 CSR:** `scripts/atlas/lib/csr-graph-artifact-v1.mjs` (+ test, 8 tests): magic/version/endian header, u32 offsets or u64 offsets (forced or automatic past 2^32 edges), u16 relation-type codes, optional f32 weights, projection/model revision and node-ordering digest in meta, `canonicalAuthority:false`, sha256 trailer, decoder rejects truncation, corruption, wrong magic/version/endianness, bad offsets, out-of-range targets. Benchmark (synthetic seeded fixture, 20,000 nodes, 400,000 edges, 2,000 random pairs): artifact 2,480,243 bytes, encode 365 ms, decode 15 ms, sorted-merge intersection 6.5 ms vs `Set` 4.2 ms, identical results. Conclusion: CSR is a compact, verifiable transport, not faster than `Set` for sparse rows (~20 neighbors); Roaring is not installed so no bitmap comparison was made (gap recorded, no install under DEPENDENCY-CAPABILITY-GUARD-01). My first benchmark attempt hung because my LCG overflowed 2^53 and the generator never produced enough distinct edges; fixed with `Math.imul`.
- **P5 Merkle:** `scripts/atlas/lib/graph-merkle-manifest-v1.mjs` (+ test, 7 tests): leaf/node/empty domain tags, order by partition id, odd-node promotion, inclusion proofs, tamper tests, `sourceAuthority:false`. Integrity of artifacts only.
- **P6 design note:** reuse `atlas_projection_outbox` (event_id, revisions, event_checksum, consumed_at, aggregate_key) and `atlas_projection_state`. Before any projection: committed canonical transaction writes the outbox row in the same transaction; event identity = event_checksum; projection generation recorded per target; consumer checkpoint = `atlas_projection_state` row per (knowledge_id, target_store); retries must be idempotent upserts keyed by canonical identity; independent readback and a coverage receipt gate `verified_at`. Blockers: registry tables absent, execution not canonical, `projection-admission` has no owner validator.
- **Tests this pass:** 23/23 across the three new files (contract 8, CSR 8, Merkle 7). Safe next commands: `node --test scripts/atlas/lib/canonical-relation-contract-v1.test.mjs scripts/atlas/lib/csr-graph-artifact-v1.test.mjs scripts/atlas/lib/graph-merkle-manifest-v1.test.mjs`.

### 6.M5 Evidence namespaces: Parent Atlas vs the legal-ai app, and a correction to 6.M4 (2026-10-27, read-only)
- **Answer:** the relation evidence I reviewed is **Parent Atlas** (code/ontology) evidence, not legal-ai case evidence. All 603 `atlas_relationship_evidence.evidence_id` values are `feature_ontology_tuples:<uuid>`; the table has an FK to `atlas_evidence` (603 rows, `evidence_kind = ontology_tuple`, all with source_revision, evidence_revision and producer_revision) and its relations are `rel:feature-ontology:*` over repository `src/...` paths. Zero of them join the legal `evidence` table (808 case-evidence rows).
- **Separation rule (verified, read-only):** no foreign key crosses between the two groups. The only FKs from outside the `atlas_*`/`graphify_*` family into it are other Parent Atlas tables (`packet_features`, `code_features`, `ontology_edges`, `feature_records`, … -> `atlas_packets`; `atlas_learned_artifact_admission_v1` -> `model_weights`). Owners: **Parent Atlas** = `atlas_*`, `graphify_*`, `code_*`, packet/feature/ontology tables; **legal-ai app** = `cases`, `evidence`, `evidence_*` (analysis cache, audit log, entities, forensic flags, tags, vectors, versions, board connections, `evidence_relationships`), `case_*`, `documents`, `legal_*`, `persons`. Names collide on the word "evidence": `atlas_evidence`/`atlas_relationship_evidence`/`atlas_evidence_entities` are Parent Atlas; `evidence*` without the `atlas_` prefix is the legal app. Chain-of-custody and admissibility rules apply to the legal tables only.
- **Rule for new work:** the canonical relation contract (6.M4) and its CSR/Merkle artifacts apply to Parent Atlas code graphs only. Legal `evidence_relationships` keep their own owner and rules (custody, SHA-256, demonstrative-reconstruction labelling); do not route them through the Atlas contract or project them to the Atlas graph without a separate, explicit decision.
- **Correction to 6.M4:** I wrote that relation evidence "lacks evidence revision, extraction recipe and span". That was wrong at the join level: `atlas_relationship_evidence.evidence_id` -> `atlas_evidence` already carries source, evidence and producer revisions on all 603 rows. Remaining real gaps: no span/offset column; every evidence row is kind `ontology_tuple`, produced by the same extractor that produced the relation, so the 6.M4 contract's independence rule would currently return `NO_INDEPENDENT_EVIDENCE` for these 603 relations; and no snapshot/scope column on `atlas_relationships`. A draft DDL extension for `atlas_relationship_evidence` is therefore not needed for revisions; do not write one until a span owner and an independent evidence source are decided.

### 6.M9 Review-sheet pipeline test and how reviewed labels feed the fan-out (2026-10-27, appended)
- **No gold labels were created by this session.** A model labeling its own candidates is not human review, so `reviewedGroup` stays empty. Pipeline test used a synthetic export (`.tmp/atlas/domain-review-v3/PIPELINE-TEST-synthetic-export-NOT-GOLD.jsonl`, reviewer `PIPELINE_TEST_NOT_A_HUMAN`, labels copied from the path signal, so circular) purely to prove the mechanics.
- **Results:** `python/atlas_domain_classifier_eval_v1.py` on the untouched candidates -> `NO_GOLD_LABELS` (143 weak-label rows, 6.3% agreement, explicitly not accuracy). On the synthetic export -> ingests 132 rows, `INSUFFICIENT_SAMPLE` (n<200, classes api/auth/database/error-handling/frontend/machine-learning/test each <30), no ECE (predictor supplies no confidence). The sheet builder accepted a subset: `node scripts/atlas/build-domain-review-sheet-v1.mjs --input <jsonl> --output .tmp/...html` -> 44 rows. The sheet's export line sets `reviewStatus:'REVIEWED'`, `reviewedGroup`, `reviewer`, `reviewedAt`, matching the harness fields (read from source; the browser UI itself was not driven).
- **Harness weakness found:** gold rows are counted from `reviewedGroup` alone and the reviewer is not checked, so a synthetic or model-written export would be counted as gold (132 gold rows above). Keep synthetic files out of any `--input` for real runs; a guard (reject `reviewer` matching a test marker, require a reviewer on every gold row, require a second reviewer on >=10%) is a recommended small change to the eval owner, not made here.
- **Cohort facts (`candidates.jsonl`, 150 rows):** all UNREVIEWED; 147 have no `packetKey`, 0 are revision-qualified (Tier B, evaluation only); 18 have no selection signal; 12 per signal domain; the selection signal is a path regex, NOT a label. 150 rows cannot reach the 200 total / 30 per class floor for 17 classes (needs >=510 labels), so no learned-model accuracy can come from this set.
- **Batch for a human:** `.tmp/atlas/domain-review-v3/batch-1-candidates.jsonl` (44 rows, stratified, <=5 per signal domain, 4 no-signal) and `review-sheet-batch-1.html`. Note: `compiler` and `gpu`/`graph`/`cache`/`retrieval` have no selection signal in this cohort, so they are absent from batch 1; the cohort needs new candidates for them.
- **Best use for the feature_label domain fan-out (recommendation):** (1) first use labels to measure the precision of the deterministic router (path rules): for each signal domain, share of rows where the reviewer agrees; rules above an agreed precision stay in DeterministicRouter, the rest become hints only. This needs only the 44-row batch and no model. (2) Use the reviewed rows as the reference set for the EMBEDDING_SIMILARITY lane (nearest reviewed neighbor), once each row has a verified chunk embedding. (3) Use the same rows to choose ConfidenceGate thresholds by observing agreement versus confidence, only when a predictor emits confidence. (4) Keep the learned classifier (DecisionTree/RandomForest/XGBoost) out until >=510 reviewed rows exist with a second-reviewer agreement check. (5) Labels are proposals attached to feature_label rows via `domain_taxonomy_v1` ids; nothing is written to `feature_domain`, `atlas_packets` or any relation table.
- Safe next commands: open `.tmp/atlas/domain-review-v3/review-sheet-batch-1.html`, label, export JSONL, then `python python/atlas_domain_classifier_eval_v1.py --input <export.jsonl> --report .tmp/atlas/<name>.json`.

Cause: the container has `WORKSPACE_ROOT` unset (helper falls back to `.`, cwd `/app`) although the repo is mounted at `/workspace` (and `python`, `models/domain-classifier` are mounted). Fix is a compose/env change (`WORKSPACE_ROOT=/workspace`) plus a container restart, which is a service lifecycle change left to the operator; not done here. Even after the fix, bound revisions would be sha256 of current worktree bytes, so they stay Tier B (not the admitted snapshot) and `packetKey` stays null until the registry exists. Its outputs share lineage with the weak labels and must not be scored as accuracy; keep it as a challenger lane behind the reviewed-label evaluation.

### 6.M12 AST/CST reference facts -> relation-evidence mapping (2026-10-27, read-only, pure)
- **Built:** `scripts/atlas/lib/structural-reference-relation-mapping-v1.mjs` (+ test, 8 tests; with the contract 16/16). Maps `StructuralReferenceFactV1` (`packages/parent-atlas/src/core/structural-symbol.ts`) to relation candidates for the 6.M4 contract: import -> `IMPORTS` (SourceFile->SourceFile, needs target source ref), export -> `EXPORTS`, call -> `CALLS`, extends -> `EXTENDS`, implements/implementation -> `IMPLEMENTS`, class_ref/type_ref -> `REFERENCES` (Symbol->Symbol). The contract gained those six structural types. Rules: only `resolution_status = canonical` facts with both endpoints become candidates (degraded/ambiguous/unresolved/missing-endpoint are typed `unresolved`, unknown kinds `unsupportedKind`, non-sha256 revisions `rejected`); origin is `AST_STRUCTURAL`; evidence = source-bytes digest (kind SOURCE_BYTES) plus the parser span refs (PARSER_SPAN), both independent of the extractor; a missing repository/snapshot scope rejects instead of defaulting; duplicate facts merge evidence, differing revisions are reported as conflicts; an optional map of existing `atlas_relationships` rows turns drift into `EXISTING_ROW_REVISION_OR_RECIPE_DIFFERS`. Output is deterministic and carries `writesPerformed:false`, `canonicalAuthority:false`. No row was inserted.
- **Live readback (read-only):** the matching owner `atlas_structural_reference_resolutions` (columns reference_id, source/target stable symbol ids, reference_kind, resolution_status, source_revision, evidence_refs) has **0 rows**, so there is nothing to map: the extraction-to-evidence bridge is still unfed in production data. `feature_structural_facts` (66,717 rows) holds only name arrays (`imports`, `calls`, `exports`) keyed by packet_key with no revision, resolution status or symbol ids, so it is not a valid input for this contract and was not mapped. `atlas_ast_nodes` has 523,271 native AST rows, but no persisted AST edge/reference table exists.
- **Remaining for `LANGEXTRACT_RELATION_EVIDENCE_BRIDGE_READ_ONLY_VERIFIED` (not reached):** a producer run that persists (or emits to a receipt) real reference facts from Tree-sitter/ast-grep with symbol resolution; scope inputs (repository UUID from `atlas_repository_identity`, snapshot UUID) which depend on the admitted snapshot; the LangExtract span-grounding check (blocked by the sidecar `WORKSPACE_ROOT` setting, 6.M10); SemanticExtractionEnvelopeV1; the BYOG fixture (GraphRAG not installed). First failing predicate unchanged: `REGISTRY_DDL_NOT_APPLIED`.
- Safe next: `node --test scripts/atlas/lib/structural-reference-relation-mapping-v1.test.mjs scripts/atlas/lib/canonical-relation-contract-v1.test.mjs`.

### 6.M15 Exact-name resolution probe over the sample's unresolved edges (2026-10-27, read-only)
- **Resolver contract read (no change):** `packages/parent-atlas/src/core/structural-reference-resolver.ts` resolves each side by (1) `atlas_symbol_versions` on `upstream_node_id` + source_revision + workspace_revision + source_ref, (2) `atlas_symbol_aliases` on `upstream-symbol:<id>`, (3) alias key equal to the text, (4) `atlas_symbol_registry.canonical_name` equal to the text. Status is `canonical` only when BOTH sides resolve through basis (1) (`upstream_node_version`); text-based matches (3, 4) can only yield `degraded`, and any side with several ids is `ambiguous`. So name matching can never produce a canonical reference by design.
- **Probe on the 20-file sample** (`.tmp/atlas/structural-reference-sample-20261027-v2.json`): 2,255 unresolved CALLS/REFERENCES edges with 340 distinct target texts; the sidecar returns the bare identifier as the target (`db`, `super`, `expect`), not a qualified name. Of 336 bare names queried against `atlas_symbol_registry.canonical_name` (active): 285 match nothing, 14 match exactly one symbol, 37 match several; against alias keys: 292 none, 16 unique, 28 ambiguous. 361 edges carry a name with exactly one registry hit, but the top "unique" hits are false positives: `expect` (vitest global, 146 edges), `source` (100) and `createHash` (node:crypto method, 33) would bind to unrelated repo symbols of the same name, while a real in-repo function (`classifyCurrentSourcesV1`, 84 edges) has 0 registry matches. Conclusion: exact bare-name matching is both incomplete (85% none) and unsafe when it does hit; do not use it to create facts. Probe script is scratch only (not committed).
- **What would work:** resolution by revision-bound node identity, i.e. an `atlas_symbol_versions` row per defining chunk (`upstream_node_id`) and an import-aware target resolver that follows `import { x } from './y'` to the defining file's symbol version. The 20-file sample shows the parser already emits `DEFINES` edges (255, resolved `native_chunk`) and `IMPORTS` edges with the import text, which are the inputs for that resolver; the registry (42,922 symbols) covers fewer symbols than `graphify_symbols` (73,309), so coverage must be measured first.
- **Status:** `LANGEXTRACT_RELATION_EVIDENCE_BRIDGE_READ_ONLY_VERIFIED` not reached. Next read-only step: measure how many of the sample's 88 IMPORTS edges resolve to a defining file under the admitted snapshot, and how many of that file's exported names have `atlas_symbol_versions` rows.

So the mismatch is a PRIOR admitted snapshot (historical), not an incompatible revision namespace; the earlier wording "incompatible" overstated it. Writers: `symbol-reconciliation-writer-v1` 32,612 rows (2026-09-13 to 2026-10-24) and `atlas-ast-symbol-version-materializer-v1` 285 rows (2026-08-24 to 2026-08-30; the 271 rows where workspace_revision equals source_revision belong to this older producer, so there that column does not hold a snapshot at all).
- **Coverage against the current admitted manifest** (execution `01a1763a…`, 29,026 root files; path forms matched exactly, no fuzzy matching): `sveltekit-frontend/…`-prefixed refs: 7,062 distinct (source_ref, source_revision, workspace_revision) triples map to a manifest path, 6,996 (99.1%) at exactly the current admitted `code_source_revision`; frontend-relative `src/…` refs: 136 triples map to a manifest path, only 9 at the admitted revision; 7,086 distinct source refs in total. So about 7,005 (path, revision) pairs line up with the current snapshot, an upper bound of roughly 24% of admitted root files; the other ~22,000 admitted files have no symbol-version row at their current bytes. Mismatches (about 66 prefixed + 127 frontend-relative triples) are files whose bytes changed since the older snapshot: historical, do not relabel.
- **Implication / proposed equivalence contract (not implemented):** a symbol-version row may be used as source-revision-bound evidence for the current snapshot when (source_ref, source_revision) equals the current admitted membership row exactly, with the path namespace mapped by an explicit, reviewed prefix rule; its `workspace_revision` stays the historical value and is never rewritten. The existing resolver's `workspace_revision = $3` predicate cannot express this and needs an owner decision. Rows with source revisions that differ stay historical. Files without rows need an authorized versioned indexing run.
- **P2 span failure root cause (fixed in the pure adapter, not the data):** `atlas-coordinate-v1.spec.ts:4` is a CRLF file. The sidecar returned the statement text with CRLF folded to LF while the line/column span still addresses the original bytes; the span itself is correct (bytes 79..497 cover the full statement). My earlier check compared unfolded strings. `rangeToVerifiedByteSpan` in `lsp-position-v1.mjs` now has an opt-in `normalizeLineEndings` that is accepted only when the folded texts are equal and is reported as `lineEndingNormalized`. Tests: lsp position 10, parity 3 (13/13). Rerun `.tmp/atlas/import-aware-audit-20261027-v2.json` (`sha256:822776a0…94c5`): 44/44 import spans verified by exact byte readback, 1 of them line-ending-normalized, 0 failures; replay digest equal (`sha256:9ff77ecb…395f`); stage counts unchanged (44 statements, 26 resolved, 40 exports identified, 6 symbol versions verified).
- **Not done:** the typed `ImportResolutionV1` contract; relation-mapper integration of verified symbol identities; Tree-sitter lexical scope analysis and LSP confirmation (the regex baseline in 6.M18 stays as the labelled heuristic); any backfill.

### 6.M27 Tree-sitter lexical scope analysis vs the regex baseline (2026-10-27, read-only)
Status: `AST_LEXICAL_SCOPE_READ_ONLY_VERIFIED` for the 20-file sample at the level of LEXICAL BINDING only (which declaration an identifier refers to inside one file). Not compiler/LSP resolution; the LSP confirmation step was NOT run. `canonicalAuthority:false`, `writesPerformed:false`; Gate V2 unchanged (FAIL).
- **Built:** `scripts/atlas/lib/ast-lexical-scope-v1.mjs` (+ test, 14 tests) on the tree-sitter grammars already shipped via `@ast-grep/napi` (no new parser). Scopes: module, function (declarations, expressions, arrows, methods), block, catch, for/for-in/for-of, class body. Bindings: imports (named, aliased, default, namespace, type-only), functions, classes, enums, types, var (hoisted to function scope), let/const (block), parameters incl. destructuring/rest/defaults, catch parameters, loop declarations. Outcomes: LOCAL_BINDING_VERIFIED, IMPORT_BINDING_VERIFIED, SHADOWED_BINDING, GLOBAL_OR_BUILTIN_CANDIDATE, MEMBER_ACCESS_UNRESOLVED, TYPE_NAMESPACE_UNRESOLVED, FREE_IDENTIFIER, PARSE_UNSUPPORTED. Spans are UTF-8 byte offsets converted from napi's UTF-16 indexes (emoji test); syntax errors and non-TS/JS languages return PARSE_UNSUPPORTED (Svelte `<script>` blocks are NOT analysed). Tests also cover closures, shadowing, hoisting, catch, optional chaining, object keys vs shorthand, replay.
- **Audit:** `scripts/atlas/ast-lexical-scope-audit-v1.mjs`; receipt `.tmp/atlas/ast-lexical-scope-audit-20261027-v2.json` (`sha256:9bc5c395…835e`), replay equal. 20/20 files at admitted revisions, 0 parse errors, 343 scopes, 448 bindings, 2,896/2,896 spans read back exactly. 2,448 identifier occurrences (every reference position, not just edge targets): local 1,035, member access 945 (unresolved by design), import binding 383, builtin/global 80, shadowed 4, free 1 (`string` in a binary expression, needs a look). The first run (receipt `…-20261027.json`) had 47 free identifiers because loop declarations (`for (const k of …)`) were not bound; that is fixed and superseded.
- **Transition matrix vs the 6.M18 regex baseline** (1,291 occurrences matched by file+name+line; the baseline receipt is kept unchanged and still labelled heuristic): regex IMPORTED 365 -> import binding 365 (agree); regex LOCAL_TOP_LEVEL 230 -> local 230; regex KNOWN_GLOBAL 25 -> builtin 25; regex FREE_NAME_UNRESOLVED 314 -> local 304, builtin 8, shadowed 1, free 1 (so about 97% of the "free" names were locals); regex LOCAL_BINDING_IN_ENCLOSING_SCOPE 113 -> local 113; regex MEMBER_ACCESS 232 -> member access 209, but 23 were actually locals (the regex misread a preceding dot). Net: the regex mis-labelled about 24% of the matched occurrences' names as free or member access; AST scope analysis removes that ambiguity.
- **Qualified symbol references (the honest count):** only the 6 SYMBOL_VERSION_VERIFIED import bindings (6.M24) have an exact symbol id. Their lexically verified call uses total 12 (all `call_expression`; 11 in spec files, 1 in `code-source-revision-packet-dry-run.ts`). A CALLS candidate also needs the calling symbol's stable id, which no source currently provides for those sites, so 0 CALLS/REFERENCES candidates were sent to the mapper and none were fabricated. File-level IMPORTS and 6 IMPORTS_SYMBOL stay as in 6.M26.
- **Not done:** compiler/LSP confirmation of the 12 uses; member access resolution (needs types); Svelte script blocks; EXTENDS/IMPLEMENTS; containing-symbol attribution. The AST step does not fix the real blocker (symbol-version coverage, snapshot UUID, `GRAPHIFY_EXECUTION_NOT_CANONICAL`, `REGISTRY_DDL_NOT_APPLIED`).
- Safe next: `node --test scripts/atlas/lib/ast-lexical-scope-v1.test.mjs`; `node scripts/atlas/ast-lexical-scope-audit-v1.mjs --out=.tmp/atlas/<new>.json`.

### 6.M32 Full Canonical Gate V2 refresh after OKF reconciliation (2026-10-27, read-only)
Ran `node scripts/validate/canonical-gate.mjs`; it produced unique run-scoped evidence under `.tmp/atlas` and made no persistent writes. Receipt `.tmp/atlas/canonical-gate-runs/2026-10-27T23-28-03.429Z-e6fcb975-bd0d-4b1d-b4f0-04626d9c5a83/canonical-gate-receipt-v2.json` has file SHA-256 `sha256:c62ae60c45242f1b5cbf803142f25f25af49b38402015026880b26bda0678330`, embedded checksum `sha256:060d5ba80ef94a505773504b49baeb0dda86a526d4a9ad1d69701137cb97d44b`, `canonicalAuthority=false`, `writesPerformed=false`, overall `FAIL`. Source snapshot and service health pass. First mandatory blocker remains `PACKET_IDENTITY/BLOCKED: GRAPHIFY_EXECUTION_NOT_CANONICAL`; execution authority is also blocked. Graph integrity fails on packet identity, feature naming, SOM coordinates, and incomplete coverage; coverage is partial (census only, no thresholds); ontology-schema fails the broad v0.2 profile (387 flagged, 356 excluded); repository coverage is partial (`REGISTRY_DDL_NOT_APPLIED`, nested scopes unmapped, authority not granted, graph coverage incomplete); projection admission is `UNKNOWN` (no current owner validator). The profile-aware OKF receipt remains distinct: its `23` owner gaps are not yet consumed by the gate's broad v0.2 evaluator. No promotion, DDL, packet/index writes, or projection mutations occurred.
- **Root cause traced (code reading only, no run):** `packages/parent-atlas/src/core/gis-canonicalization.ts` lines 82-93 push a symbol version ONLY inside the branch `resolution.status === 'unresolved' && promote_unresolved === true && symbol_resolver.promote`. `symbol-reconciliation-writer-v1.mts` supplies `promote` (`repo.promoteNomination`, which inserts `atlas_symbol_versions` at `symbol-registry-repository.ts:194`) only with `--apply --allow-create`. In the dry run all 32,184 nominations resolve `canonical` (basis `exact_symbol_key`, via the `upstream-symbol:<id>` alias), 0 are unresolved. Therefore an apply would take the promote branch for none of them: expected `symbol_version_count` stays 0 and no `atlas_symbol_versions` row would be created at workspace revision `c63620c4…`. (Inference from code plus the dry-run counts; not proven by an apply, which is not authorized.)
- **Conclusion:** no writer found creates a version row for an already-canonical symbol at a NEW workspace revision. The two version writers are `promoteNomination` (unresolved symbols only) and `materialize-ast-symbol-versions.mjs` (legacy AST materializer, 285 rows, its own comments record a prior defect). That is the structural reason symbol-version coverage for the current snapshot is 0 at `c63620c4…` and only ~24% by exact (path, source_revision) match to older rows. Closing the gap needs an owner-designed, separately authorized version-materialization path (revision-bound, idempotent insert-or-verify keyed by stable symbol id + source_revision), not an `--apply` of the existing writer. Open question for the registry owner: should a version row be per workspace revision, or per source revision only (which would make unchanged files' old rows valid across snapshots, matching the 6.M23 proposed equivalence rule)?

### 6.M35 Svelte <script> lexical scope support (2026-10-27, read-only, lexical only)
Status: `scripts/atlas/lib/svelte-script-scope-v1.mjs` (+ test, 5 tests) extracts `<script>` blocks from the exact source (TS or JS, module flag), runs the 6.M27 lexical analysis per block and shifts spans to absolute UTF-8 byte offsets in the .svelte file. Svelte 5 runes (`$state`, `$derived`, `$effect`, `$props`, `$bindable`, `$inspect`, `$host`) are reported as compiler globals with a note, not as free names. The template (`{…}`, directives) is NOT analysed, so template-only names are never reported as free or resolved (`templateAnalyzed:false`); instance and module script scopes are analysed separately (a name declared in the module script reads as free in the instance script, a known limit). Syntax errors return a typed SYNTAX_ERROR.
- **Run on the 10 admitted `.svelte` files** under `sveltekit-frontend/src/lib/components/ui/` (read-only, inline run, no receipt file): 10 script blocks, 0 unsupported, 215 bindings (30 imports), 532 identifier occurrences: local 281, member access 130, builtin/global/rune 77, import binding 18, type name unresolved 16, free 10; 747/747 spans read back exactly against the file bytes. This closes the earlier gap that the sidecar produced no import edges for these files: lexical import bindings now exist for them (30 imports), but they were not fed through ImportResolutionV1, symbol versions or the relation mapper, and `.svelte` files have 0 symbol-version coverage (6.M29), so no Svelte relation candidates exist.
- **Not done:** wiring Svelte imports into `import-resolution-contract-v1` (needs Svelte import edges or a script-text parse), template identifiers, LSP via `svelteserver`.

### 6.M48 Svelte 5 template chunk extractor and template-to-script reference resolver (lexical, read-only; commit 2f4a1c26efb)

Combined focused tests 15/15.
- **Corpus run (all 937 `.svelte` files under `sveltekit-frontend/src`, excluding node_modules/_archive/.svelte-kit):** 937 parsed, 0 parse errors; chunks 7,813 (if 5,794, each 1,779, render 189, snippet 48, key 3, await 0); references 41,947 with 0 span mismatches: script import 4,752, script declaration 24,320, template local 11,699, builtin candidate 1,010, store candidate 149, free 17 (browser globals `alert`, `confirm`, `prompt`, `history`, `AbortSignal`, `TextDecoder`, plus `handleSubmit` in `NESElementsShowcase.svelte`).
- **Bugs found by that run and fixed:** `for (const x of ...)` declarations were not bound; declared names were recorded as references (inflated 42,028 to 41,947).
- **Newer stack comparison (isolated container, sidecar untouched):** treesitter-chunker 6.0.0 + tree-sitter 0.26.0 + pack 1.20.0 fixes only the CRLF `script_element` span; block extents, snippet and render remain missing. Pack 1.22.1 is not installable with chunker 6.0.0. The sidecar `Dockerfile` pins 4.0.0/0.25.2/0.9.0 (lines 40-42). No upgrade performed.
- **Limits:** lexical only; no member-access resolution; not confirmed by the Svelte language server (template definition queries time out per §6.M47); `<style lang="scss">` not handled. Both extractors are `proposalOnly` / `canonicalAuthority: false`.
- **Open ownership question:** the chunk extractor now emits template identifier occurrences (§6.M47) and `svelte-template-references-v1` also resolves them. One owner should be chosen before either is wired into a pipeline.
- **Overlap measured (937 files, read-only, matched on exact UTF-8 byte span):** chunk extractor `template_identifier_occurrence` 59,515; reference resolver 41,947; same span in both 35,012 (83% of the resolver's references); only in the chunk extractor 24,503 (sampled: member property names such as `preventDefault`, handler-local parameters such as `e`, i.e. unresolved occurrences, not references); only in the resolver 6,935 (sampled: component tag names such as `POIPhotoModalImpl` and `class:` directive shorthand, which the chunk extractor does not report). Reading: the chunk extractor is the occurrence/structure producer, the resolver is the scope-resolution layer; they can be joined by span.
- **Full classification of the non-overlapping sets (same 937 files):** only in the chunk extractor 24,517: member property names 22,330 (91%; text preceded by `.`), object keys 929 (4%), other 1,258 (5%; arrow-function parameter declarations such as `(e) =>`, `(_, i)`, and TypeScript type names such as `HTMLSelectElement` in `as` casts). None of these is a reference, so their exclusion from the resolver is correct; the chunk extractor's `template_identifier_occurrence` name overstates what it holds. Only in the resolver 6,935 by context: component tag names 4,084, `class:` directives 1,199, `{@const}` initialisers 588, each-key expressions 451, transition directives 203, `{@render}` callee 189 (the chunk extractor reports these as a separate `render_reference` observation type), spread 97, `use:` 83, tag 36, key 3, animate 2. By outcome: script import 4,309, script declaration 1,322, template local 1,256, builtin candidate 36, store candidate 12. Decision input: no conflicting ownership; join by byte span; the chunk extractor supplies occurrences and structure, the resolver supplies scope resolution and covers component tags, directives, `{@const}` and each-key expressions that the chunk extractor omits. The occurrence classification of member/key/declaration positions is a text-adjacency heuristic, not an AST classification.
- Unverified: a claimed `sveltejs/tree-sitter-svelte` "June 2026 fork" from a pasted note; a web search found no such repository. No canonical or datastore writes; Canonical Gate V2 remains FAIL.

### 6.M53 Agreement-based structural evidence fusion; Svelte 5 rune facts (read-only)
- **Added:** `scripts/atlas/lib/structural-evidence-fusion-v1.mjs` + test (7/7) and `scripts/atlas/audit-svelte-rune-fusion-v1.mjs`. Fusion keys facts by (kind, name, startByte); statuses `AGREED_INDEPENDENT`, `SINGLE_GROUP`, `CONFLICT` (same key, different end spans). Independence groups must be declared: ast-grep CLI and the repo's ast-grep napi libraries share one tree-sitter grammar and count as ONE witness; the Svelte compiler estree is the other group. No scores, no confidence, no authority. This is not retrieval fusion (SearchRuntime owns RRF).
- **Result (937 components, 886 with runes; compiler parse errors 0):** 6,202 rune-call facts: `$state` 4,059, `$derived` 819, `$props` 709, `$effect` 482, `$bindable` 133 (`$inspect`/`$host` none). **All 6,202 AGREED_INDEPENDENT; 0 CONFLICT; 0 SINGLE_GROUP.** Two runs, identical digest `sha256:ce6d1155…ef5`. Receipt `.tmp/atlas/svelte-rune-fusion-20261028.json` (sha256:82467a07…5d7f).
- **Notes:** ast-grep CLI exits 1 on a no-match pattern (treated as empty); the binary is called directly (no shell) because `$` and parentheses are shell-sensitive. Negative control: with the compiler source removed, the same facts report `SINGLE_GROUP`; an end-span mismatch reports `CONFLICT`.
- **Limits:** `*.svelte` only (not `.svelte.ts`/`.svelte.js`); rune calls only, not imports, scopes or template structure; agreement of two parsers on a span is not symbol identity and not compiler/LSP name resolution. Canonical Gate V2 remains FAIL; no writes.

- FIXED (2026-10-28, commit 2ee74ea6b68): `ReferenceError: props is not defined` on `/demos/page-layouts`, `/demos/ui-components`, `/demos/unified-dashboard`. Root cause (found by pausing the browser at the throw via CDP and reading the compiled module): `node_modules/@sveltejs/kit/src/runtime/components/svelte-5/layout.svelte` (SvelteKit's default layout, inserted for a layout level with `+layout.ts` and no `+layout.svelte`, here `routes/(app)/demos/`) was compiled in legacy mode, where `$props` is a store auto-subscription to an undefined `props`. Cause in `svelte.config.js`: `vitePlugin.dynamicCompileOptions` forced `runes: false` for every `node_modules` file except `svelte-sonner` and `bits-ui`. Fix: also exclude `@sveltejs/kit`. Regression spec `sveltekit-frontend/src/lib/config/svelte-compile-options.spec.ts` (4/4 pass; against the previous config the `@sveltejs/kit` case returns `{runes:false}`, so it fails there). Browser check after the fix (13 routes, GET only): 0 page errors and 0 console errors on the three routes; the ten previously clean routes still clean. REVIEW_REQUIRED: `/demos/page-layouts` now logs one aborted `__data.json?x-sveltekit-invalidated=...` request (`ERR_ABORTED`), probably the test teardown cancelling an in-flight invalidation; not investigated. Other lessons: Git Bash rewrites a leading-slash CLI argument (`/demos/x`) into a Windows path unless `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1`; Vitest specs here need `// @vitest-environment node` (jsdom breaks esbuild).

Practical path: use a Mixamo donor character (for example Y Bot) for skeleton and the clips already in `ANIM_TYPE_MAP`, and attach each jester part as a rigid mesh to a bone (Babylon `attachToBone`), instead of skinning. No asset was copied into the repo and no clip was downloaded.
- **Service key correction:** `hooks.server.ts` is deny-by-default for `/api/*` and does not read `x-service-key`, so the `summarize-indexed-packets-dual-llm.mjs` header only helps in `npm run dev` (`DEV_BYPASS_AUTH`) or with a session cookie; the script comment now says so. G4 note: `/api/metrics` is a PUBLIC prefix; `api/metrics/retrieval` now requires a user (spec 2/2). The other `CLAUDE.md` "unauthenticated" G4 routes (`batch-summary/jobs`, `retrieval/dual-lane`, `telemetry/implementation-clusters`, `phase102/retrieval-pipeline`, `retrieval/cache-layers/*`) sit under the hook's authenticated default, so they are not anonymous; only role checks remain.

- **Courtroom rig compatibility finding (2026-10-28, read-only GLB inspection):** `static/models/courtroom/character_alice.glb` (2.2 MB, 408 nodes, 289 joints, 4 skins, 3 meshes) is **not a Mixamo rig**: it is a VRM-style skeleton (`J_Bip_C_Hips`, `J_Bip_C_Head`, 92 `J_Bip_*` humanoid bones plus `J_Sec_*` secondary skirt/hair bones). The clip packs (checked `run_injured.glb`) animate **76 distinct nodes with non-Mixamo names** (`Hips`, `Head`, `LeftArm`, `RightFinger1Metacarpal`, ...; clip names carry `mvn` motion-capture tags). Only **17 of the 76** animated node names also exist in `character_alice.glb`, so the clips cannot drive that character by name matching alone, and `ImportAnimationsAsync` would apply few or none of the tracks. Consequence for the jester plan: before any `attachToBone` wiring, a bone-name retarget table (clip skeleton to `J_Bip_*`) and a rest-pose check (A-pose vs T-pose) are required, or the clips must be retargeted in Blender. Not built. Decision needed: retarget map plus rest-pose check in code, or a Blender retarget pass.

(1) Babylon's glTF loader auto-starts the first animation group on import; fixed by setting `animationStartMode = 0` through `SceneLoader.OnPluginActivatedObservable`. With that, `retargetPose` maps rest to rest on the real assets (16 mapped bone pairs, 0 bones off rest, 0 deg). (2) The scene shows each clip armature in its frame-0 mocap pose, not its bind pose, so the source rest must come from the clip GLB's raw node rotations (JSON chunk; 64 of 67 rig nodes patched). GLB JSON: node `Face` (7 primitives) and `Hair` (1) use skin 0 (289 joints) with `JOINTS_0`/`WEIGHTS_0`; node **`Body` (mesh `Body (merged)`, 12 primitives, 8,432 vertices: 3364+700+346+1784+631+373+102+276+656+50+61+89) has no `skin`, no `JOINTS_0`, no `WEIGHTS_0`**. Babylon agrees: all 8 skinned meshes are Hair/Face on the one 289-bone skeleton (62 mapped bones linked to the written nodes), the 12 `Body_primitive*` meshes are unskinned children of `Body` under `__root__`. So the body, dress, arms and legs are rigid geometry that no bone transform can move, which is why the render stays in the T-pose while the head and hair move and why the node-level limb directions can agree with the clip (0 deg) without any visible arm motion. Three.js also reported 8 skinned meshes (same 8). This is an asset defect, not a retargeting defect: the retarget bridge is correct at node level (bind-pose source, no auto-play), but the character needs its Body skinned to the armature (Blender auto-weights, or programmatic rigid/nearest-bone weights written as `JOINTS_0`/`WEIGHTS_0` plus a `skin` on the Body node), or a different character with a fully skinned body. Not done. Jester `attachToBone` is separate and could proceed on bone nodes, but its visual verification needs a skinned body or at least the clip armature visible. `objection_dramatic` (null targets) and `walk_normal` (0 resolved) remain broken.

**Pitfalls learned**
- Git Bash rewrites a leading-slash CLI argument into a Windows path: use `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` when passing `/models/...` or `/demos/...` to node.
- Playwright `networkidle` never settles on these pages; wait for a selector instead. A parallel route sweep gives false failures (first-compile latency, aborted requests): sweep sequentially with a fresh context.
- `static/models` is gitignored: GLB-based specs must `skipIf` the files are absent.
- Babylon's glTF loader auto-starts the first animation group and shows each clip's frame-0 pose: set `animationStartMode = 0` and take the bind pose from the clip GLB's node data.
- `ImportAnimationsAsync` binds by node name; Mixamo clips (`mixamorig:*`) cannot bind to a `J_Bip_*` character, which is why they threw; use the GLB sampler path.
- `character_alice.glb` Body mesh is unskinned (only Face and Hair are); use the skinned copy.
- `hooks.server.ts` is deny-by-default for `/api/*` and ignores `x-service-key`.
- A `CLAUDE.md`/`claude.md` case difference exists on Windows; `git add claude.md`.

**Authorizations used:** additive DDL and `CREATE INDEX` on the two tables (operator: "ddl as long as not dropping tables"); `:8090` restart with the vision profile; no pushes; no canonical row writes; `error_logs` read only.
- **E2B browser eval (2026-10-28; authorized by operator; `:8090` stopped with `/slots` idle, restarted with `ornith-1.5-vlm`, `/props` shows vision true):** headless Chromium on the real RTX 3060 Ti (adapter nvidia/ampere, `shader-f16`, maxBufferSize 2 GiB), local `static/gemma4_e2b_onnx` q4f16 assets (decoder 1.5 GB + embed 1.6 GB) served by redirecting transformers.js local-model requests, huggingface.co blocked (0 remote requests). Two real defects found in `lib/ai/e2b`: (1) `session.ts` called `adapter.requestAdapterInfo()`, removed from current Chrome, so WebGPU always looked unavailable and E2B never loaded; now uses `adapter.info` with the old call as fallback. (2) `inference.ts` hand-built a Gemma 3 `<start_of_turn>` prompt; on Gemma 4 the model echoed the prompt and looped. With messages passed (model's own chat template): hearsay definition correct in 12.6 s (cold, includes shader compile), one-word emotion `Disbelief` in 0.56 s, three prosecutor duties in 5.0 s (60-token cap, greedy). `inference.ts` now passes chat messages. Not measured: tokens/s with a proper counter, memory peak, EmbeddingGemma ONNX on WebGPU, courtroom-sim integration. Scratch: `.tmp/atlas/e2b-eval-*.json`, `.tmp/atlas/e2b-eval-*.mjs`. Donor checkpoints `gemma4_assistant_huggingface_7_3_26` and `gemma4-e4b-qat-assistant-2026-07-20` are tiny ranker donors (159 MB), not the E2B generator.

**Pitfalls found this segment:** Vite HMR full reloads (from another session's edits) kill Playwright evals; retry or block `/@vite/client`. `Glob` on `~/Downloads` recurses into huge trees; use `ls -t | head` and `find -maxdepth 1`. Bash heredocs containing both quotes and backticks can fail whole commands; use the file tools. Downloaded kits are untrusted data: read, do not run. `work_state_read` is an agent tool, not a shell command. `getBindGroups ... reading '0'` on Babylon WebGPU means a material/effect failed to compile, not an MSAA mismatch (the scene already sets `antialias:false`).

**Authorizations used:** E2B eval with `:8090` stop and restart; seeding one labeled fictional case and starting simulation sessions; additive DDL on `error_logs`; copying from `~/Downloads`; no pushes; no canonical row writes outside those.
